1	To assess whether the DDR three-way partition generalizes beyond the default temperature ($\tau{=}0.7$), we computed per-feature Cohen's $d$ (generation~1 vs.\ generation~2+) at $\tau \in \{0.5, 0.7, 0.9\}$ for all four generators in the temperature ablation (12 conditions total; Table~\ref{tab:ddr-temp-stability}).
2	
3	\begin{table}[ht]
4	\centering
5	\small
6	\caption{Mean $|d|$ (gen-1 vs.\ gen-2+) across 4 generators at each temperature. Partition labels: \textbf{D}rifting ($|d|>0.5$), \textbf{I}ntermediate ($0.1{<}|d|{<}0.5$), near-\textbf{S}tationary ($|d|{<}0.1$). Boldface marks features that shift from their $\tau{=}0.7$ partition.}
7	\label{tab:ddr-temp-stability}
8	\resizebox{\linewidth}{!}{%
9	\begin{tabular}{lccc l}
10	\toprule
11	\textbf{Feature} & $\boldsymbol{\tau{=}0.5}$ & $\boldsymbol{\tau{=}0.7}$ & $\boldsymbol{\tau{=}0.9}$ & \textbf{Group} \\
12	\midrule
13	Entropy mean  & 1.39\,\textsuperscript{D} & 1.10\,\textsuperscript{D} & 0.58\,\textsuperscript{D} & Drifting \\
14	TTR           & 1.00\,\textsuperscript{D} & 0.85\,\textsuperscript{D} & \textbf{0.45}\,\textsuperscript{I} & Drifting \\
15	Hapax ratio   & 0.87\,\textsuperscript{D} & 0.75\,\textsuperscript{D} & \textbf{0.42}\,\textsuperscript{I} & Drifting \\
16	\midrule
17	Entropy std   & \textbf{0.84}\,\textsuperscript{D} & 0.52\,\textsuperscript{D} & \textbf{0.20}\,\textsuperscript{I} & Intermediate \\
18	\midrule
19	Entropy max   & 0.06\,\textsuperscript{S} & 0.09\,\textsuperscript{S} & \textbf{0.11}\,\textsuperscript{I} & Stationary \\
20	Tail decay    & 0.07\,\textsuperscript{S} & \textbf{0.12}\,\textsuperscript{I} & \textbf{0.15}\,\textsuperscript{I} & Stationary \\
21	Entropy min   & 0.04\,\textsuperscript{S} & 0.06\,\textsuperscript{S} & 0.04\,\textsuperscript{S} & Stationary \\
22	\bottomrule
23	\end{tabular}}
24	\end{table}
25	
26	The partition anchors are robust: entropy mean remains drifting in all 12 conditions, TTR in 10/12, and hapax ratio in 9/12; entropy min and entropy max stay near-stationary in 10/12 and 9/12 conditions, respectively.
27	The two boundary features are more temperature-sensitive: entropy std is drifting at $\tau{=}0.5$ for all generators but falls to intermediate at $\tau{=}0.9$ for three of four, while tail decay fluctuates between near-stationary and intermediate across generators.
28	Effect sizes compress monotonically with temperature: for the fixed drifting set (entropy mean, TTR, hapax ratio), mean $|d|$ drops from 1.09 at $\tau{=}0.5$ to 0.90 at $\tau{=}0.7$ to 0.48 at $\tau{=}0.9$, shifting individual features across partition boundaries at high temperature without altering the qualitative hierarchy: lexical diversity features drift fastest, entropy extrema remain stable across all conditions tested.
29	
```

1	\subsection*{Instruction-Tuned Model Results}
2	
3	Table~\ref{tab:instruct} summarizes depth classification under instruction tuning for three model families.
4	All results use within-model 5-fold CV on single-generator chains; LOGO evaluation is available for two models but uses earlier generator subsets (5--6 generators), so cross-model numbers are not directly comparable across rows.
5	
6	\begin{table}[ht]
7	\centering
8	\small
9	\caption{Depth classification: base vs.\ instruction-tuned models (within-model 5-fold CV). $\rho$: Spearman correlation of entropy mean with depth. Rev.: number of features (out of 7) whose $\rho$ reverses sign under instruction tuning.}
10	\label{tab:instruct}
11	\resizebox{\linewidth}{!}{%
12	\begin{tabular}{lcc cc cc c}
13	\toprule
14	& \multicolumn{2}{c}{\textbf{3-class F1}} & \multicolumn{2}{c}{\textbf{Binary F1}} & \multicolumn{2}{c}{$\boldsymbol{\rho}$\textbf{(ent.\ mean)}} & \\
15	\cmidrule(lr){2-3} \cmidrule(lr){4-5} \cmidrule(lr){6-7}
16	\textbf{Model} & Base & Instruct & Base & Instruct & Base & Instruct & \textbf{Rev.} \\
17	\midrule
18	OLMo-2-1B       & 0.754 & 0.575 & 0.845 & 0.813 & $-$0.64 & $+$0.48 & 6/7 \\
19	Qwen3-1.7B      & 0.769 & 0.732 & 0.861 & 0.875 & $-$0.60 & $+$0.48 & 6/7 \\
20	Llama-3.2-3B    & 0.742 & 0.544 & 0.849 & 0.838 & $-$0.63 & $+$0.23 & 3/7 \\
21	\bottomrule
22	\end{tabular}}
23	\end{table}
24	
25	Instruction tuning degrades 3-class F1 by 3.7--19.9\,pp across all three families, while binary F1 (gen-1 vs.\ gen-2+) changes remain small ($|\Delta| \leq 3.2$\,pp).
26	The asymmetry indicates that instruction tuning primarily disrupts the gen-0/gen-1 boundary (human vs.\ first-generation), consistent with RLHF shifting the output distribution away from human-like text.
27	Entropy-mean $\rho$ reverses sign for all three models ($-0.60$--$-0.64$ base $\rightarrow$ $+0.23$--$+0.48$ instruct), and 6 of 7 features reverse for OLMo and Qwen vs.\ 3 of 7 for Llama.
28	This pattern is consistent with instruction tuning shifting the generation attractor rather than eliminating iterative dynamics (\S\ref{sec:discussion}).
29	
30	\paragraph{Retraining on instruction-tuned data.}
31	To test whether the depth signal persists under instruction tuning with reversed polarity, we retrain the XGBoost classifier on instruction-tuned feature data (Table~\ref{tab:instruct-retrain}).
32	
33	\begin{table}[ht]
34	\centering
35	\small
36	\caption{Classification recovery under instruction-tuned retraining (binary-only: gen-1 vs.\ gen-2+, gen-0 excluded from training and evaluation). Base$\rightarrow$Inst: base-trained classifier tested on instruct data. Note: Table~\ref{tab:instruct} reports higher instruct binary F1 (e.g., OLMo 0.813) because it uses 3-class training with binary evaluation, where gen-0 in training helps the classifier learn the feature space.}
37	\label{tab:instruct-retrain}
38	\begin{tabular}{lccc}
39	\toprule
40	\textbf{Model} & \textbf{Base$\rightarrow$Inst} & \textbf{Retrained} & \textbf{$\Delta$} \\
41	\midrule
42	OLMo-2-1B-Inst   & 0.321 & 0.590 & $+$0.269 \\
43	Qwen3-1.7B-Inst  & 0.500 & 0.660 & $+$0.160 \\
44	Qwen3-0.6B-Inst  & 0.532 & 0.600 & $+$0.068 \\
45	\bottomrule
46	\end{tabular}
47	\end{table}
48	
49	Retraining recovers substantial within-model performance ($+$6.8--26.9\,pp), but the retrained F1 (0.590--0.660) remains below base-model levels (0.69--0.72).
50	Three-generator instruct LOGO yields binary F1\,=\,0.487, indicating weak cross-generator transfer under instruction tuning.
51	
52	\paragraph{DDR structure under instruction tuning.}
53	$k{=}2$ clustering in $(|\rho|, d)$ space on pooled instruct features yields silhouette\,=\,0.372 (vs.\ 0.660 for base models), indicating that the clean drifting/non-drifting partition is substantially compressed.
54	The compression is model-dependent: OLMo-Instruct shows near-zero $|\rho|$ for TTR and hapax (0.006, 0.025), while Qwen instruct models retain moderate correlations ($|\rho|{=}0.48$--$0.53$).
55	Cross-condition transfer is near chance in both directions (base$\rightarrow$instruct binary F1\,=\,0.490; instruct$\rightarrow$base F1\,=\,0.454), with the asymmetry ($-$0.036) suggesting that instruct-trained features generalize even less to base distributions than the reverse.
56	These results indicate that instruction tuning compresses the feature-space structure underlying DDR rather than simply reversing feature directions (Figure~\ref{fig:instruct-ddr}).
57	
58	\begin{figure}[H]
59	\centering
60	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth, trim=0 0 205pt 0, clip]{fig_appendix_instruct_ddr.pdf}\\[-2pt]
61	{\small (a) Base models (silhouette\,=\,0.660)}
62	\caption{DDR partition under base vs.\ instruction-tuned models. Bars show pooled $|d|$; markers show individual generators.}
63	\label{fig:instruct-ddr}
64	\end{figure}
65	
66	\begin{figure}[H]
67	\addtocounter{figure}{-1}
68	\centering
69	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth, trim=205pt 0 0 0, clip]{fig_appendix_instruct_ddr.pdf}\\[-2pt]
70	{\small (b) Instruction-tuned models (silhouette\,=\,0.372)}
71	\caption{(Continued) Instruction tuning compresses all features toward zero, collapsing the partition. Entropy max shifts from near-stationary to intermediate; formerly drifting features (hapax, TTR) lose most of their effect size.}
72	\end{figure}
73	
74	\paragraph{Reference model sensitivity.}
75	One hypothesis for the instruct degradation is reference-model mismatch: the base Pythia-410M reference may produce systematically biased NLL estimates on instruction-tuned outputs, masking depth-dependent variation.
76	To test this, we replace the reference with Qwen3-0.6B-Instruct and recompute all features on the two instruct generators.
77	The hypothesis is partially rejected: Qwen3-1.7B-Instruct shows mixed changes (entropy max $d$: $0.51 \to 0.08$, becoming near-stationary; entropy std $d$: $1.07 \to 1.51$, increasing), while OLMo-Instruct loses nearly all depth signal (entropy mean $d$: $1.33 \to 0.02$; tail decay $d$: $0.60 \to 0.06$).
78	The model-dependent heterogeneity rules out reference mismatch as a universal explanation for instruct DDR degradation, supporting the interpretation that alignment fundamentally reshapes the generation dynamics rather than introducing a correctable measurement artifact.
79	
80	\paragraph{End-to-end instruct classification.}
81	To test whether depth signal persists at the token level despite summary-statistic degradation, we apply the QLoRA end-to-end classifier (Appendix~\ref{app:qlora}) to instruction-tuned data using within-model 5-fold stratified CV (Table~\ref{tab:instruct-qlora}).
82	
83	\begin{table}[ht]
84	\centering
85	\small
86	\caption{End-to-end QLoRA vs.\ XGBoost on instruction-tuned data (within-model 5-fold CV, Qwen3-0.6B backbone). Base QLoRA rows shown for reference only; instruct sets ($N{\approx}4.4$--$5.0$K) are approximately twice the base sets ($N{\approx}2.3$K), so cross-condition comparison is confounded by training set size.}
87	\label{tab:instruct-qlora}
88	\resizebox{\linewidth}{!}{%
89	\begin{tabular}{lccccc}
90	\toprule
91	& \multicolumn{2}{c}{\textbf{3-class F1}} & & \\
92	\cmidrule(lr){2-3}
93	\textbf{Model} & XGB & QLoRA & $\boldsymbol{\Delta}$ & \textbf{$N$} \\
94	\midrule
95	OLMo-2-1B-Inst   & 0.575 & 0.888\,$\pm$\,0.007 & $+$31.3\,pp & 4{,}429 \\
96	Qwen3-1.7B-Inst  & 0.732 & 0.907\,$\pm$\,0.008 & $+$17.5\,pp & 4{,}963 \\
97	\midrule
98	OLMo-2-1B (base) &       & 0.859\,$\pm$\,0.004 &             & 2{,}379 \\
99	Qwen3-1.7B (base)&       & 0.904\,$\pm$\,0.009 &             & 2{,}291 \\
100	\bottomrule
101	\end{tabular}}
102	\end{table}
103	
104	The XGBoost-to-QLoRA gap on the same instruct data is substantial ($+$17.5 to $+$31.3\,pp), indicating that depth signal persists at the token level under instruction tuning but is inaccessible to the seven summary statistics; the degradation reported in Table~\ref{tab:instruct} reflects a feature-level limitation rather than signal absence.
105	Note that instruct samples are approximately twice the base count used here (Table~\ref{tab:instruct-qlora}), so the instruct-vs-base QLoRA comparison is confounded by training set size; the meaningful comparison is QLoRA-vs-XGBoost on the same instruct data.
106	All results use within-model 5-fold CV, not cross-generator LOGO, limiting generalization claims.

Base QLoRA rows shown for reference only; instruct sets ($N{\\approx}4.4$--$5.0$K) are approximately twice the base sets ($N{\\approx}2.3$K), so cross-condition comparison is confounded by training set size.}\n\\label{tab:instruct-qlora}\n\\resizebox{\\linewidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccc}\n\\toprule\n& \\multicolumn{2}{c}{\\textbf{3-class F1}} & & \\\\\n\\cmidrule(lr){2-3}\n\\textbf{Model} & XGB & QLoRA & $\\boldsymbol{\\Delta}$ & \\textbf{$N$} \\\\\n\\midrule\nOLMo-2-1B-Inst   & 0.575 & 0.888\\,$\\pm$\\,0.007 & $+$31.3\\,pp & 4{,}429 \\\\\nQwen3-1.7B-Inst  & 0.732 & 0.907\\,$\\pm$\\,0.008 & $+$17.5\\,pp & 4{,}963 \\\\\n\\midrule\nOLMo-2-1B (base) &       & 0.859\\,$\\pm$\\,0.004 &             & 2{,}379 \\\\\nQwen3-1.7B (base)&       & 0.904\\,$\\pm$\\,0.009 &             & 2{,}291 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\nThe XGBoost-to-QLoRA gap on the same instruct data is substantial ($+$17.5 to $+$31.3\\,pp), indicating that depth signal persists at the token level under instruction tuning but is inaccessible to the seven summary statistics; the degradation reported in Table~\\ref{tab:instruct} reflects a feature-level limitation rather than signal absence.\nNote that instruct samples are approximately twice the base count used here (Table~\\ref{tab:instruct-qlora}), so the instruct-vs-base QLoRA comparison is confounded by training set size; the meaningful comparison is QLoRA-vs-XGBoost on the same instruct data.\nAll results use within-model 5-fold CV, not cross-generator LOGO, limiting generalization claims.\n",
      "numLines": 107,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 107
    }

1	This appendix visualizes per-feature convergence trajectories across generation depth, complementing the DDR analysis in \S\ref{sec:results-differential}.
2	
3	\begin{figure}[H]
4	\centering
5	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{fig_convergence_dynamics.pdf}
6	\caption{Per-feature convergence dynamics across generation depths 0--4, averaged over 8 generators. Each feature is expressed as Cohen's $d$ relative to depth~0, so all features start at zero and the $y$-axis directly reflects effect size. (a)~Drifting features (hapax ratio, TTR, entropy mean) and the intermediate feature (entropy std) diverge monotonically; lexical diversity features (TTR, hapax ratio) form a fast-convergence group ($|d| > 2$ by depth~2), while entropy mean and entropy std drift more slowly. (b)~Near-stationary features (entropy max, tail decay, entropy min) remain within $|d| < 0.25$ across all depths, confirming location stability. Shaded bands indicate $\pm 1$ standard deviation across generators.}
7	\label{fig:convergence-dynamics}
8	\end{figure}
9

    "originalFile": "\\subsection*{Instruction-Tuned Model Results}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:instruct} summarizes depth classification under instruction tuning for three model families.\nAll results use within-model 5-fold CV on single-generator chains; LOGO evaluation is available for two models but uses earlier generator subsets (5--6 generators), so cross-model numbers are not directly comparable across rows.\n\n\\begin{table}[ht]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Depth classification: base vs.\\ instruction-tuned models (within-model 5-fold CV). $\\rho$: Spearman correlation of entropy mean with depth. Rev.: number of features (out of 7) whose $\\rho$ reverses sign under instruction tuning.}\n\\label{tab:instruct}\n\\resizebox{\\linewidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{lcc cc cc c}\n\\toprule\n& \\multicolumn{2}{c}{\\textbf{3-class F1}} & \\multicolumn{2}{c}{\\textbf{Binary F1}} & \\multicolumn{2}{c}{$\\boldsymbol{\\rho}$\\textbf{(ent.\\ mean)}} & \\\\\n\\cmidrule(lr){2-3} \\cmidrule(lr){4-5} \\cmidrule(lr){6-7}\n\\textbf{Model} & Base & Instruct & Base & Instruct & Base & Instruct & \\textbf{Rev.} \\\\\n\\midrule\nOLMo-2-1B       & 0.754 & 0.575 & 0.845 & 0.813 & $-$0.64 & $+$0.48 & 6/7 \\\\\nQwen3-1.7B      & 0.769 & 0.732 & 0.861 & 0.875 & $-$0.60 & $+$0.48 & 6/7 \\\\\nLlama-3.2-3B    & 0.742 & 0.544 & 0.849 & 0.838 & $-$0.63 & $+$0.23 & 3/7 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\nInstruction tuning degrades 3-class F1 by 3.7--19.9\\,pp across all three families, while binary F1 (gen-1 vs.\\ gen-2+) changes remain small ($|\\Delta| \\leq 3.2$\\,pp).\nThe asymmetry indicates that instruction tuning primarily disrupts the gen-0/gen-1 boundary (human vs.\\ first-generation), consistent with RLHF shifting the output distribution away from human-like text.\nEntropy-mean $\\rho$ reverses sign for all three models ($-0.60$--$-0.64$ base $\\rightarrow$ $+0.23$--$+0.48$ instruct), and 6 of 7 features reverse for OLMo and Qwen vs.\\ 3 of 7 for Llama.\nThis pattern is consistent with instruction tuning shifting the generation attractor rather than eliminating iterative dynamics (\\S\\ref{sec:discussion}).\n\n\\paragraph{Retraining on instruction-tuned data.}\nTo test whether the depth signal persists under instruction tuning with reversed polarity, we retrain the XGBoost classifier on instruction-tuned feature data (Table~\\ref{tab:instruct-retrain}).\n\n\\begin{table}[ht]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Classification recovery under instruction-tuned retraining (binary-only: gen-1 vs.\\ gen-2+, gen-0 excluded from training and evaluation). Base$\\rightarrow$Inst: base-trained classifier tested on instruct data. Note: Table~\\ref{tab:instruct} reports higher instruct binary F1 (e.g., OLMo 0.813) because it uses 3-class training with binary evaluation, where gen-0 in training helps the classifier learn the feature space.}\n\\label{tab:instruct-retrain}\n\\begin{tabular}{lccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Model} & \\textbf{Base$\\rightarrow$Inst} & \\textbf{Retrained} & \\textbf{$\\Delta$} \\\\\n\\midrule\nOLMo-2-1B-Inst   & 0.321 & 0.590 & $+$0.269 \\\\\nQwen3-1.7B-Inst  & 0.500 & 0.660 & $+$0.160 \\\\\nQwen3-0.6B-Inst  & 0.532 & 0.600 & $+$0.068 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nRetraining recovers substantial within-model performance ($+$6.8--26.9\\,pp), but the retrained F1 (0.590--0.660) remains below base-model levels (0.69--0.72).\nThree-generator instruct LOGO yields binary F1\\,=\\,0.487, indicating weak cross-generator transfer under instruction tuning.\n\n\\paragraph{DDR structure under instruction tuning.}\n$k{=}2$ clustering in $(|\\rho|, d)$ space on pooled instruct features yields silhouette\\,=\\,0.372 (vs.\\ 0.660 for base models), indicating that the clean drifting/non-drifting partition is substantially compressed.\nThe compression is model-dependent: OLMo-Instruct shows near-zero $|\\rho|$ for TTR and hapax (0.006, 0.025), while Qwen instruct models retain moderate correlations ($|\\rho|{=}0.48$--$0.53$).\nCross-condition transfer is near chance in both directions (base$\\rightarrow$instruct binary F1\\,=\\,0.490; instruct$\\rightarrow$base F1\\,=\\,0.454), with the asymmetry ($-$0.036) suggesting that instruct-trained features generalize even less to base distributions than the reverse.\nThese results indicate that instruction tuning compresses the feature-space structure underlying DDR rather than simply reversing feature directions (Figure~\\ref{fig:instruct-ddr}).\n\n\\begin{figure}[H]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth, trim=0 0 205pt 0, clip]{fig_appendix_instruct_ddr.pdf}\\\\[-2pt]\n{\\small (a) Base models (silhouette\\,=\\,0.660)}\n\\caption{DDR partition under base vs.\\ instruction-tuned models. Bars show pooled $|d|$; markers show individual generators.}\n\\label{fig:instruct-ddr}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\begin{figure}[H]\n\\addtocounter{figure}{-1}\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth, trim=205pt 0 0 0, clip]{fig_appendix_instruct_ddr.pdf}\\\\[-2pt]\n{\\small (b) Instruction-tuned models (silhouette\\,=\\,0.372)}\n\\caption{(Continued) Instruction tuning compresses all features toward zero, collapsing the partition. Entropy max shifts from near-stationary to intermediate; formerly drifting features (hapax, TTR) lose most of their effect size.}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{Reference model sensitivity.}\nOne hypothesis for the instruct degradation is reference-model mismatch: the base Pythia-410M reference may produce systematically biased NLL estimates on instruction-tuned outputs, masking depth-dependent variation.\nTo test this, we replace the reference with Qwen3-0.6B-Instruct and recompute all features on the two instruct generators.\nThe hypothesis is partially rejected: Qwen3-1.7B-Instruct shows mixed changes (entropy max $d$: $0.51 \\to 0.08$, becoming near-stationary; entropy std $d$: $1.07 \\to 1.51$, increasing), while OLMo-Instruct loses nearly all depth signal (entropy mean $d$: $1.33 \\to 0.02$; tail decay $d$: $0.60 \\to 0.06$).\nThe model-dependent heterogeneity rules out reference mismatch as a universal explanation for instruct DDR degradation, supporting the interpretation that alignment fundamentally reshapes the generation dynamics rather than introducing a correctable measurement artifact.\n\n\\paragraph{End-to-end instruct classification.}\nTo test whether depth signal persists at the token level despite summary-statistic degradation, we apply the QLoRA end-to-end classifier (Appendix~\\ref{app:qlora}) to instruction-tuned data using within-model 5-fold stratified CV (Table~\\ref{tab:instruct-qlora}).\n\n\\begin{table}[ht]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{End-to-end QLoRA vs.\\ XGBoost on instruction-tuned data (within-model 5-fold CV, Qwen3-0.6B backbone). Base QLoRA rows shown for reference only; instruct sets ($N{\\approx}4.4$--$5.0$K) are approximately twice the base sets ($N{\\approx}2.3$K), so cross-condition comparison is confounded by training set size.}\n\\label{tab:instruct-qlora}\n\\resizebox{\\linewidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccc}\n\\toprule\n& \\multicolumn{2}{c}{\\textbf{3-class F1}} & & \\\\\n\\cmidrule(lr){2-3}\n\\textbf{Model} & XGB & QLoRA & $\\boldsymbol{\\Delta}$ & \\textbf{$N$} \\\\\n\\midrule\nOLMo-2-1B-Inst   & 0.575 & 0.888\\,$\\pm$\\,0.007 & $+$31.3\\,pp & 4{,}429 \\\\\nQwen3-1.7B-Inst  & 0.732 & 0.907\\,$\\pm$\\,0.008 & $+$17.5\\,pp & 4{,}963 \\\\\n\\midrule\nOLMo-2-1B (base) &       & 0.859\\,$\\pm$\\,0.004 &             & 2{,}379 \\\\\nQwen3-1.7B (base)&       & 0.904\\,$\\pm$\\,0.009 &             & 2{,}291 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\nThe XGBoost-to-QLoRA gap on the same instruct data is substantial ($+$17.5 to $+$31.3\\,pp), indicating that depth signal persists at the token level under instruction tuning but is inaccessible to the seven summary statistics; the degradation reported in Table~\\ref{tab:instruct} reflects a feature-level limitation rather than signal absence.\nNote that instruct samples are approximately twice the base count used here (Table~\\ref{tab:instruct-qlora}), so the instruct-vs-base QLoRA comparison is confounded by training set size; the meaningful comparison is QLoRA-vs-XGBoost on the same instruct data.\nAll results use within-model 5-fold CV, not cross-generator LOGO, limiting generalization claims.\n",

          "-\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth, trim=205pt 0 0 0, clip]{fig_appendix_instruct_ddr.pdf}\\\\[-2pt]",
          "-{\\small (b) Instruction-tuned models (silhouette\\,=\\,0.372)}",
          "-\\caption{(Continued) Instruction tuning compresses all features toward zero, collapsing the partition. Entropy max shifts from near-stationary to intermediate; formerly drifting features (hapax, TTR) lose most of their effect size.}",
          "-\\end{figure}",
          "-",
          " \\paragraph{Reference model sensitivity.}",
          " One hypothesis for the instruct degradation is reference-model mismatch: the base Pythia-410M reference may produce systematically biased NLL estimates on instruction-tuned outputs, masking depth-dependent variation.",
          " To test this, we replace the reference with Qwen3-0.6B-Instruct and recompute all features on the two instruct generators."
        ]
      }
    ],

{
  "parentUuid": "1422ed1f-1486-472a-bb88-899678453527",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "attachment": {
    "type": "file",
    "filename": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_3/projects/gen-depth-fingerprint/docs/paper/appendix_instruct.tex",
    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_3/projects/gen-depth-fingerprint/docs/paper/appendix_instruct.tex",
        "content": "\\subsection*{Instruction-Tuned Model Results}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:instruct} summarizes depth classification under instruction tuning for three model families.\nAll results use within-model 5-fold CV on single-generator chains; LOGO evaluation is available for two models but uses earlier generator subsets (5--6 generators), so cross-model numbers are not directly comparable across rows.\n\n\\begin{table}[ht]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Depth classification: base vs.\\ instruction-tuned models (within-model 5-fold CV). $\\rho$: Spearman correlation of entropy mean with depth. Rev.: number of features (out of 7) whose $\\rho$ reverses sign under instruction tuning.}\n\\label{tab:instruct}\n\\resizebox{\\linewidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{lcc cc cc c}\n\\toprule\n& \\multicolumn{2}{c}{\\textbf{3-class F1}} & \\multicolumn{2}{c}{\\textbf{Binary F1}} & \\multicolumn{2}{c}{$\\boldsymbol{\\rho}$\\textbf{(ent.\\ mean)}} & \\\\\n\\cmidrule(lr){2-3} \\cmidrule(lr){4-5} \\cmidrule(lr){6-7}\n\\textbf{Model} & Base & Instruct & Base & Instruct & Base & Instruct & \\textbf{Rev.} \\\\\n\\midrule\nOLMo-2-1B       & 0.754 & 0.575 & 0.845 & 0.813 & $-$0.64 & $+$0.48 & 6/7 \\\\\nQwen3-1.7B      & 0.769 & 0.732 & 0.861 & 0.875 & $-$0.60 & $+$0.48 & 6/7 \\\\\nLlama-3.2-3B    & 0.742 & 0.544 & 0.849 & 0.838 & $-$0.63 & $+$0.23 & 3/7 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\nInstruction tuning degrades 3-class F1 by 3.7--19.9\\,pp across all three families, while binary F1 (gen-1 vs.\\ gen-2+) changes remain small ($|\\Delta| \\leq 3.2$\\,pp).\nThe asymmetry indicates that instruction tuning primarily disrupts the gen-0/gen-1 boundary (human vs.\\ first-generation), consistent with RLHF shifting the output distribution away from human-like text.\nEntropy-mean $\\rho$ reverses sign for all three models ($-0.60$--$-0.64$ base $\\rightarrow$ $+0.23$--$+0.48$ instruct), and 6 of 7 features reverse for OLMo and Qwen vs.\\ 3 of 7 for Llama.\nThis pattern is consistent with instruction tuning shifting the generation attractor rather than eliminating iterative dynamics (\\S\\ref{sec:discussion}).\n\n\\paragraph{Retraining on instruction-tuned data.}\nTo test whether the depth signal persists under instruction tuning with reversed polarity, we retrain the XGBoost classifier on instruction-tuned feature data (Table~\\ref{tab:instruct-retrain}).\n\n\\begin{table}[ht]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Classification recovery under instruction-tuned retraining (binary-only: gen-1 vs.\\ gen-2+, gen-0 excluded from training and evaluation). Base$\\rightarrow$Inst: base-trained classifier tested on instruct data. Note: Table~\\ref{tab:instruct} reports higher instruct binary F1 (e.g., OLMo 0.813) because it uses 3-class training with binary evaluation, where gen-0 in training helps the classifier learn the feature space.}\n\\label{tab:instruct-retrain}\n\\begin{tabular}{lccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Model} & \\textbf{Base$\\rightarrow$Inst} & \\textbf{Retrained} & \\textbf{$\\Delta$} \\\\\n\\midrule\nOLMo-2-1B-Inst   & 0.321 & 0.590 & $+$0.269 \\\\\nQwen3-1.7B-Inst  & 0.500 & 0.660 & $+$0.160 \\\\\nQwen3-0.6B-Inst  & 0.532 & 0.600 & $+$0.068 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nRetraining recovers substantial within-model performance ($+$6.8--26.9\\,pp), but the retrained F1 (0.590--0.660) remains below base-model levels (0.69--0.72).\nThree-generator instruct LOGO yields binary F1\\,=\\,0.487, indicating weak cross-generator transfer under instruction tuning.\n\n\\paragraph{DDR structure under instruction tuning.}\n$k{=}2$ clustering in $(|\\rho|, d)$ space on pooled instruct features yields silhouette\\,=\\,0.372 (vs.\\ 0.660 for base models), indicating that the clean drifting/non-drifting partition is substantially compressed.\nThe compression is model-dependent: OLMo-Instruct shows near-zero $|\\rho|$ for TTR and hapax (0.006, 0.025), while Qwen instruct models retain moderate correlations ($|\\rho|{=}0.48$--$0.53$).\nCross-condition transfer is near chance in both directions (base$\\rightarrow$instruct binary F1\\,=\\,0.490; instruct$\\rightarrow$base F1\\,=\\,0.454), with the asymmetry ($-$0.036) suggesting that instruct-trained features generalize even less to base distributions than the reverse.\nThese results indicate that instruction tuning compresses the feature-space structure underlying DDR rather than simply reversing feature directions (Figure~\\ref{fig:instruct-ddr}).\n\n\\begin{figure}[H]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{fig_appendix_instruct_ddr.pdf}\n\\caption{DDR partition under base vs.\\ instruction-tuned models. (a)~Base models (silhouette\\,=\\,0.660): clear drifting/non-drifting separation. (b)~Instruction-tuned models (silhouette\\,=\\,0.372): all features compress toward zero, collapsing the partition; entropy max shifts from near-stationary to intermediate; formerly drifting features (hapax, TTR) lose most of their effect size. Bars show pooled $|d|$; markers show individual generators.}\n\\label{fig:instruct-ddr}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{Reference model sensitivity.}\nOne hypothesis for the instruct degradation is reference-model mismatch: the base Pythia-410M reference may produce systematically biased NLL estimates on instruction-tuned outputs, masking depth-dependent variation.\nTo test this, we replace the reference with Qwen3-0.6B-Instruct and recompute all features on the two instruct generators.\nThe hypothesis is partially rejected: Qwen3-1.7B-Instruct shows mixed changes (entropy max $d$: $0.51 \\to 0.08$, becoming near-stationary; entropy std $d$: $1.07 \\to 1.51$, increasing), while OLMo-Instruct loses nearly all depth signal (entropy mean $d$: $1.33 \\to 0.02$; tail decay $d$: $0.60 \\to 0.06$).\nThe model-dependent heterogeneity rules out reference mismatch as a universal explanation for instruct DDR degradation, supporting the interpretation that alignment fundamentally reshapes the generation dynamics rather than introducing a correctable measurement artifact.\n\n\\paragraph{End-to-end instruct classification.}\nTo test whether depth signal persists at the token level despite summary-statistic degradation, we apply the QLoRA end-to-end classifier (Appendix~\\ref{app:qlora}) to instruction-tuned data using within-model 5-fold stratified CV (Table~\\ref{tab:instruct-qlora}).\n\n\\begin{table}[ht]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{End-to-end QLoRA vs.\\ XGBoost on instruction-tuned data (within-model 5-fold CV, Qwen3-0.6B backbone). Base QLoRA rows shown for reference only; instruct sets ($N{\\approx}4.4$--$5.0$K) are approximately twice the base sets ($N{\\approx}2.3$K), so cross-condition comparison is confounded by training set size.}\n\\label{tab:instruct-qlora}\n\\resizebox{\\linewidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccc}\n\\toprule\n& \\multicolumn{2}{c}{\\textbf{3-class F1}} & & \\\\\n\\cmidrule(lr){2-3}\n\\textbf{Model} & XGB & QLoRA & $\\boldsymbol{\\Delta}$ & \\textbf{$N$} \\\\\n\\midrule\nOLMo-2-1B-Inst   & 0.575 & 0.888\\,$\\pm$\\,0.007 & $+$31.3\\,pp & 4{,}429 \\\\\nQwen3-1.7B-Inst  & 0.732 & 0.907\\,$\\pm$\\,0.008 & $+$17.5\\,pp & 4{,}963 \\\\\n\\midrule\nOLMo-2-1B (base) &       & 0.859\\,$\\pm$\\,0.004 &             & 2{,}379 \\\\\nQwen3-1.7B (base)&       & 0.904\\,$\\pm$\\,0.009 &             & 2{,}291 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\nThe XGBoost-to-QLoRA gap on the same instruct data is substantial ($+$17.5 to $+$31.3\\,pp), indicating that depth signal persists at the token level under instruction tuning but is inaccessible to the seven summary statistics; the degradation reported in Table~\\ref{tab:instruct} reflects a feature-level limitation rather than signal absence.\nNote that instruct samples are approximately twice the base count used here (Table~\\ref{tab:instruct-qlora}), so the instruct-vs-base QLoRA comparison is confounded by training set size; the meaningful comparison is QLoRA-vs-XGBoost on the same instruct data.\nAll results use within-model 5-fold CV, not cross-generator LOGO, limiting generalization claims.\n",
        "numLines": 98,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 98
      }
    },

        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_3/projects/gen-depth-fingerprint/docs/paper/discussion.tex",
        "content": "\\vspace{-2mm}\n\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\vspace{-2mm}\n\n%% Mechanism — why DDR exists\n\\looseness=-1 We hypothesize that DDR reflects a structural property of base-model autoregressive generation: recursive inputs compress the token-level NLL distribution monotonically (Appendix~\\ref{app:nll-distribution}), producing location drift independent of the token-selection mechanism.\nControlled decoding ablations support this: greedy decoding preserves the full partition---shape features remain near-stationary (mean $|d|{=}0.05$) while drifting features maintain large effects (mean $d{=}0.94$; Appendix~\\ref{app:decoding-strategy})---confirming DDR is not a sampling artifact; temperature dose-response ($\\tau{=}0.5{\\to}0.9$) modulates drift magnitude while preserving the partition (Appendix~\\ref{app:sampling-sensitivity}); semantic-constraint attenuation confirms predictions (Appendix~\\ref{app:prompt-type}).\nANOVA variance decomposition quantifies this asymmetry: $\\eta^2_{\\text{depth}}{=}0.34$--$0.37$ for location vs.\\ ${<}0.01$ for shape (${\\geq}50{\\times}$; Appendix~\\ref{app:variance-decomposition}).\n\n%% Implications — what DDR enables\n\\looseness=-1 Depth constitutes a new analytical dimension for base-model corpus auditing that binary human-vs-machine classifiers cannot access.\nEven with limited per-instance recall, the classifier supports batch contamination estimation to ${\\pm}3$--$4$\\% accuracy at $n{=}1{,}000$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:error-analysis}); depth-stratified curation has been proposed as a prophylactic against model collapse \\cite{drayson_detection_prevents_collapse}, though no downstream improvement was observed (Appendix~\\ref{app:downstream}).\n\n%% Scope \\& Connections — where DDR works and fails\n\\looseness=-1 DDR is mechanistically distinct from model collapse \\cite{curse_of_recursion,strong_model_collapse}: retraining erodes tail coverage via weight updates, whereas inference-chain drift shifts only location (Appendix~\\ref{app:ddr-supplement}); DDR complements \\citeauthor{broken_telephone}'s factuality degradation and \\citeauthor{markovian_chains}'s convergence dynamics (Appendix~\\ref{app:iterative-comparison}); all three analyses observe qualitative saturation within a similar depth range (${\\sim}$3--5 iterations), consistent with the mixing property of finite-vocabulary autoregressive processes.\n\\looseness=-1 Instruction tuning compresses DDR asymmetrically (6/7 features show $\\rho$ reversal; Appendix~\\ref{app:instruct}); QLoRA recovers to F1\\,=\\,0.89--0.91, suggesting depth signal persists in higher-dimensional representations; whether this compression reflects RLHF mode-seeking behavior or SFT diversity effects remains open.\nThree depth classes represent the current practical resolution limit (Appendix~\\ref{app:finer-depth}); cross-domain signal persists (F1\\,=\\,0.74--0.75; Appendix~\\ref{app:domain}) and degrades gracefully under token replacement ($-$4.6\\,pp at 5\\%, F1\\,=\\,0.614 at 10\\%; Appendix~\\ref{app:human-edit}).\n",
        "numLines": 19,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 19
      }
    },

      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/agent-ml-research/skills/global/review-report-action.skill.md",
        "content": "---\nname: review-report-action\ndescription: '当 Research Director 收到 Review 阶段回传的审稿报告时,立即使用本 skill。先做 Holistic Pass(识别 SYSTEMIC_ISSUE),再做 Per-Comment Action Matrix。禁止\"已知悉\"整体回复。'\nalways: false\nroles:\n  - director\n---\n\n# Review Report Action Matrix\n\n## 硬约束\n收到审稿报告后**禁止**整体性 \"已知悉\" 回复。必须**先做 Holistic Pass,再做 Per-Comment Action Matrix**。\n\n## Step 1: Holistic Pass（整体审视，action matrix 之前必做）\n\n审稿意见不是独立的,经常多条指向同一个系统性问题。切碎成多条独立 action 会漏掉根因。\n\n1. **聚类**：把所有审稿意见按主题聚类（method design / experiment design / claim strength / writing clarity / novelty / related work）\n2. **识别系统性问题**：任何一个 cluster 含 ≥ 3 条意见 → 标记为 `[SYSTEMIC_ISSUE]`,不再切碎成独立 action,作为单独根因处理\n3. **矩阵回溯**：如果 SYSTEMIC_ISSUE 指向 core method design → 立即触发 `experiment-matrix` skill 回顾:\n   - 现有实验矩阵在新的 method 理解下还有几格有效？\n   - 哪些之前的 positive 实验可能因此失效（method 基础变了）？\n   - 哪些格子要重新跑？\n4. **全局影响评估**：SYSTEMIC_ISSUE 是否影响论文 core claim？如果是 → 必须子调 `red-section` 做 framing 评估,然后再动 action matrix\n\n**Holistic Pass 输出写入 ROADMAP.md 的\"审稿回溯·系统性问题\"section**,先于 action matrix。\n\n## Step 2: Action Matrix 格式\n\n| 审稿意见 ID | 内容摘要 | 类别 | 动作 | 负责人 | ETA |\n|-----------|--------|------|------|-------|-----|\n| R1.1 | 缺某 ablation | 补实验 | 让 Agent 铺 exp_XXX | Agent | 2d |\n| R1.2 | Claim 过强 | 改 paper | 调 red-section 重写 intro | Agent | 1d |\n| R1.3 | 某 method 解释不清 | 写 rebuttal | Director 写 rebuttal 论点 | Director | 1d |\n| R2.1 | Core claim 不成立 | framing 调整 | 子调 red-section + 改矩阵 | Agent + Director | 5d |\n\n## 类别\n- **补实验**：必须让 Agent 铺实验（可与 rebuttal 并行）\n- **改 paper**：让 Agent 调 red-section 后改章节\n- **写 rebuttal**：Director 主笔\n- **拒绝**：有理由的拒绝（写入 action matrix 的\"拒绝理由\"字段）\n\n## 产出\n- action matrix 写入 ROADMAP.md 的\"审稿回溯\"section\n- 决策日志同步（`log_decision`）\n- 补实验条目立即调度（不等 rebuttal 写完）\n- 如果有 \"framing 调整\" 类别 → 子调 red-section skill\n\n## Step 3: 同步 Issue Tracker（必做）\n\naction matrix 定稿后,同步更新跨轮次 Issue Tracker `artifacts/review_issues.yaml`:\n\n1. 用 `read_local_file(\"projects/{project}/artifacts/review_issues.yaml\")` 读当前 tracker（不存在则视为空）\n2. 对 action matrix 中每条:\n   - 找到对应的 issue ID（同一 cluster / 相似 summary）\n   - 把 `status` 从 `open` 翻到 `in_progress`\n   - 在 `action_items` 里追加本轮 action（含 round / action / assigned_to / status）\n3. 对上轮标 `in_progress` 且本轮 Reviewer 已确认修复（`[RESOLVED]`）的:\n   - `status` 改为 `resolved`,记 `resolution_round` 和 `resolution`\n4. 调 `update_review_issues(project, issues_yaml=完整 YAML)` 写回\n\n## 提交下一轮审稿（如适用）\n\n调 `submit_paper_to_review` 时根据情景设 `recommended_review_mode`:\n\n- **`continue`**：刚迭代修了上轮问题,需要 Reviewer 带着上轮记忆验证修复效果\n- **`fresh`**：做了重大 reframing / 距上次审稿 >48h / 论文结构大改 → 想要不受干扰的客观评估\n\n同时在 `mode_reason` 写一句推荐理由。人类仍可在审批卡片上覆盖。\n",
        "numLines": 70,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 70
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../../../../agent-ml-research/skills/global/review-report-action.skill.md"
  },

```text
1	To isolate the depth signal from generator identity, we train a binary classifier (gen-1 vs.\ gen-2+) on each of the eight generators independently and evaluate on all others.
2	Table~\ref{tab:transfer} reports the full 8$\times$8 transfer matrix and Figure~\ref{fig:transfer} visualizes it as a heatmap.
3	Note that these classifiers are trained with a binary objective, whereas the LOGO classifiers in Table~\ref{tab:logo-results} are trained on a 3-class task and evaluated on the binary subtask; the two setups are therefore not directly comparable.
4	
5	\begin{table}[ht]
6	\centering
7	\scriptsize
8	\setlength{\tabcolsep}{3pt}
9	\caption{Cross-generator binary F1 transfer matrix (gen-1 vs.\ gen-2+). Each row trains on one generator; each column evaluates on another. Diagonal entries (\underline{underlined}) are within-generator. All 56 off-diagonal pairs exceed chance (0.5). Classifiers use the same 7-feature XGBoost configuration as LOGO but are trained with a binary objective.}
10	\label{tab:transfer}
11	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{%
12	\begin{tabular}{l|cccccccc}
13	\toprule
14	\textbf{Train $\backslash$ Test} & \textbf{Py-2.8B} & \textbf{Py-1B} & \textbf{Qw-0.6B} & \textbf{Qw-1.7B} & \textbf{Qw-8B} & \textbf{OLMo-1B} & \textbf{Ll-3B} & \textbf{Mi-7B} \\
15	\midrule
16	Py-2.8B  & \underline{0.985} & 0.873 & 0.823 & 0.833 & 0.741 & 0.829 & 0.815 & 0.712 \\
17	Py-1B    & 0.834 & \underline{0.987} & 0.828 & 0.821 & 0.704 & 0.778 & 0.764 & 0.620 \\
18	Qw-0.6B  & 0.786 & 0.846 & \underline{0.991} & 0.851 & 0.786 & 0.795 & 0.760 & 0.680 \\
19	Qw-1.7B  & 0.794 & 0.839 & 0.859 & \underline{0.989} & 0.790 & 0.817 & 0.798 & 0.717 \\
20	Qw-8B   & 0.811 & 0.836 & 0.853 & 0.855 & \underline{0.984} & 0.848 & 0.839 & 0.790 \\
21	OLMo-1B  & 0.811 & 0.827 & 0.837 & 0.852 & 0.803 & \underline{0.988} & 0.828 & 0.763 \\
22	Ll-3B    & 0.856 & 0.860 & 0.845 & 0.851 & 0.798 & 0.851 & \underline{0.987} & 0.787 \\
23	Mi-7B    & 0.834 & 0.837 & 0.837 & 0.845 & 0.817 & 0.849 & 0.851 & \underline{0.969} \\
24	\bottomrule
25	\end{tabular}}
26	\end{table}
27	
28	\begin{figure}[H]
29	\centering
30	\includegraphics[width=0.85\columnwidth]{fig_transfer_heatmap.pdf}
31	\caption{Heatmap of the cross-generator binary F1 transfer matrix (Table~\ref{tab:transfer}). Same-family pairs (e.g., Pythia$\leftrightarrow$Pythia, Qwen$\leftrightarrow$Qwen) show slightly higher transfer; Mi-7B is the hardest transfer target (column minimum 0.62) yet remains well above chance.}
32	\label{fig:transfer}
33	\end{figure}
34	
35	All 56 off-diagonal transfer pairs yield F1 above chance, with a mean of 0.810 (95\% bootstrap CI [0.796, 0.822]; one-sample $t=46.58$, $p < 10^{-10}$).
36	Transfer is strongest within families (e.g., Py-2.8B$\to$Py-1B: 0.87) and weakest for Py-1B$\to$Mi-7B (0.62), consistent with the scale and reference-affinity effects discussed in \S\ref{sec:results-differential}.
37	These results confirm that depth signatures generalize across architectures, parameter scales, and model families, providing evidence against generator identity as a primary confound.
38	
```

1	To assess whether the DDR three-way partition generalizes beyond the default temperature ($\tau{=}0.7$), we computed per-feature Cohen's $d$ (generation~1 vs.\ generation~2+) at $\tau \in \{0.5, 0.7, 0.9\}$ for all four generators in the temperature ablation (12 conditions total; Table~\ref{tab:ddr-temp-stability}).

7	\label{tab:ddr-temp-stability}
8	\resizebox{\linewidth}{!}{%
9	\begin{tabular}{lccc l}

14	TTR           & 1.00\,\textsuperscript{D} & 0.85\,\textsuperscript{D} & \textbf{0.45}\,\textsuperscript{I} & Drifting \\
15	Hapax ratio   & 0.87\,\textsuperscript{D} & 0.75\,\textsuperscript{D} & \textbf{0.42}\,\textsuperscript{I} & Drifting \\
16	\midrule
17	Entropy std   & \textbf{0.84}\,\textsuperscript{D} & 0.52\,\textsuperscript{D} & \textbf{0.20}\,\textsuperscript{I} & Intermediate \\
18	\midrule

20	Tail decay    & 0.07\,\textsuperscript{S} & \textbf{0.12}\,\textsuperscript{I} & \textbf{0.15}\,\textsuperscript{I} & Stationary \\
21	Entropy min   & 0.04\,\textsuperscript{S} & 0.06\,\textsuperscript{S} & 0.04\,\textsuperscript{S} & Stationary \\

25	
26	The partition anchors are robust: entropy mean remains drifting in all 12 conditions, TTR in 10/12, and hapax ratio in 9/12; entropy min and entropy max stay near-stationary in 10/12 and 9/12 conditions, respectively.
27	The two boundary features are more temperature-sensitive: entropy std is drifting at $\tau{=}0.5$ for all generators but falls to intermediate at $\tau{=}0.9$ for three of four, while tail decay fluctuates between near-stationary and intermediate across generators.
28	Effect sizes compress monotonically with temperature: for the fixed drifting set (entropy mean, TTR, hapax ratio), mean $|d|$ drops from 1.09 at $\tau{=}0.5$ to 0.90 at $\tau{=}0.7$ to 0.48 at $\tau{=}0.9$, shifting individual features across partition boundaries at high temperature without altering the qualitative hierarchy: lexical diversity features drift fastest, entropy extrema remain stable across all conditions tested.

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01X732utmb4X3W7PVrJJs7aq`</summary>

```text
1	This appendix visualizes per-feature convergence trajectories across generation depth, complementing the DDR analysis in \S\ref{sec:results-differential}.
2	
3	\begin{figure}[H]
4	\centering
5	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{fig_convergence_dynamics.pdf}
6	\caption{Per-feature convergence dynamics across generation depths 0--4, averaged over 8 generators. Each feature is expressed as Cohen's $d$ relative to depth~0, so all features start at zero and the $y$-axis directly reflects effect size. (a)~Drifting features (hapax ratio, TTR, entropy mean) and the intermediate feature (entropy std) diverge monotonically; lexical diversity features (TTR, hapax ratio) form a fast-convergence group ($|d| > 2$ by depth~2), while entropy mean and entropy std drift more slowly. (b)~Near-stationary features (entropy max, tail decay, entropy min) remain within $|d| < 0.25$ across all depths, confirming location stability. Shaded bands indicate $\pm 1$ standard deviation across generators.}
7	\label{fig:convergence-dynamics}
8	\end{figure}
9	
```

1	\begingroup
2	\setlength{\parskip}{2pt plus 1pt}
3	
4	\subsection*{DDR Trend Lines}
5	
6	Figure~\ref{fig:ddr-trends} shows the per-feature depth trend (mean across all samples at each depth) with individual generator traces overlaid.
7	The consistent downward trajectories for drifting features across all generators confirm that DDR is a universal property of recursive generation.
8	Shape features exhibit flat trajectories regardless of generator, with near-zero $R^2$ for the linear fit.
9	$R^2$ values are computed from ordinary least-squares regression on depth-level means (five points per generator, one per depth 0--4), not per-sample regression; the annotated Spearman $\rho$ provides the complementary per-sample monotonicity measure.
10	
11	\begin{figure*}[t]
12	\centering
13	\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{fig_appendix_ddr_trends.pdf}
14	\caption{Per-feature depth trends. Bold line: pooled mean ($\pm$1.96\,SE shading) across all generators, colored by DDR zone (blue: drifting, wine: intermediate, amber: near-stationary). Thin traces: individual generators. Spearman $\rho$ and linear $R^2$ are annotated per panel.}
15	\label{fig:ddr-trends}
16	\end{figure*}
17	
18	\subsection*{UMAP Embedding}
19	
20	Figure~\ref{fig:umap} shows a 2D UMAP projection of the 7-feature space (7{,}500 stratified samples, StandardScaler normalization, $k$\,=\,30 neighbors, min\_dist\,=\,0.3).
21	Panel~(a) reveals a depth gradient from gen-0 through gen-4, with progressive overlap at deeper generations consistent with the convergence that motivates the gen-2+ class collapse.
22	Panel~(b) shows the 3-class view: gen-0 forms a separable cluster, while gen-1 and gen-2+ overlap substantially, explaining the classification difficulty at this boundary.
23	
24	\endgroup
25	
26	\begin{figure}[H]
27	\centering
28	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth, trim=0 0 356pt 0, clip]{fig_appendix_umap.pdf}\\[-2pt]
29	{\small (a) Colored by raw generation depth (0--4)}\\[6pt]
30	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth, trim=356pt 0 0 0, clip]{fig_appendix_umap.pdf}\\[-2pt]
31	{\small (b) Colored by 3-class label}
32	\caption{UMAP 2D projection of the 7-feature space ($N$\,=\,7{,}500 stratified sample). (a) A gradient structure emerges, with deeper depths converging. (b) Gen-0 separates clearly; gen-1 and gen-2+ overlap, consistent with the high false-negative rate at the gen-1/gen-2+ boundary.}
33	\label{fig:umap}
34	\end{figure}
35

Then Figure 8 (the large `figure*[t]` from feature_distributions) is on page 35. The remaining causality text (just 8 lines) sits on page 34 with nothing after it.
 
The root cause is that `\FloatBarrier` after the causality section is preventing Figure 8 from floating back to fill the gap on page 34. If I remove it, the variance decomposition text would flow onto page 34 after the Mistral paragraph, and Figure 8 would float to wherever it naturally fits in the document.

    "originalFile": "\\begingroup\n\\setlength{\\parskip}{2pt plus 1pt}\n\n\\subsection*{DDR Trend Lines}\n\nFigure~\\ref{fig:ddr-trends} shows the per-feature depth trend (mean across all samples at each depth) with individual generator traces overlaid.\nThe consistent downward trajectories for drifting features across all generators confirm that DDR is a universal property of recursive generation.\nShape features exhibit flat trajectories regardless of generator, with near-zero $R^2$ for the linear fit.\n$R^2$ values are computed from ordinary least-squares regression on depth-level means (five points per generator, one per depth 0--4), not per-sample regression; the annotated Spearman $\\rho$ provides the complementary per-sample monotonicity measure.\n\n\\begin{figure}[H]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{fig_appendix_ddr_trends.pdf}\n\\caption{Per-feature depth trends. Bold line: pooled mean ($\\pm$1.96\\,SE shading) across all generators, colored by DDR zone (blue: drifting, wine: intermediate, amber: near-stationary). Thin traces: individual generators. Spearman $\\rho$ and linear $R^2$ are annotated per panel.}\n\\label{fig:ddr-trends}\n\\end{figure*}\n\n\\subsection*{UMAP Embedding}\n\nFigure~\\ref{fig:umap} shows a 2D UMAP projection of the 7-feature space (7{,}500 stratified samples, StandardScaler normalization, $k$\\,=\\,30 neighbors, min\\_dist\\,=\\,0.3).\nPanel~(a) reveals a depth gradient from gen-0 through gen-4, with progressive overlap at deeper generations consistent with the convergence that motivates the gen-2+ class collapse.\nPanel~(b) shows the 3-class view: gen-0 forms a separable cluster, while gen-1 and gen-2+ overlap substantially, explaining the classification difficulty at this boundary.\n\n\\endgroup\n\n\\begin{figure}[H]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth, trim=0 0 356pt 0, clip]{fig_appendix_umap.pdf}\\\\[-2pt]\n{\\small (a) Colored by raw generation depth (0--4)}\\\\[6pt]\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth, trim=356pt 0 0 0, clip]{fig_appendix_umap.pdf}\\\\[-2pt]\n{\\small (b) Colored by 3-class label}\n\\caption{UMAP 2D projection of the 7-feature space ($N$\\,=\\,7{,}500 stratified sample). (a) A gradient structure emerges, with deeper depths converging. (b) Gen-0 separates clearly; gen-1 and gen-2+ overlap, consistent with the high false-negative rate at the gen-1/gen-2+ boundary.}\n\\label{fig:umap}\n\\end{figure}\n",

    "originalFile": "\\begingroup\n\\setlength{\\parskip}{2pt plus 1pt}\n\n\\subsection*{DDR Trend Lines}\n\nFigure~\\ref{fig:ddr-trends} shows the per-feature depth trend (mean across all samples at each depth) with individual generator traces overlaid.\nThe consistent downward trajectories for drifting features across all generators confirm that DDR is a universal property of recursive generation.\nShape features exhibit flat trajectories regardless of generator, with near-zero $R^2$ for the linear fit.\n$R^2$ values are computed from ordinary least-squares regression on depth-level means (five points per generator, one per depth 0--4), not per-sample regression; the annotated Spearman $\\rho$ provides the complementary per-sample monotonicity measure.\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{fig_appendix_ddr_trends.pdf}\n\\caption{Per-feature depth trends. Bold line: pooled mean ($\\pm$1.96\\,SE shading) across all generators, colored by DDR zone (blue: drifting, wine: intermediate, amber: near-stationary). Thin traces: individual generators. Spearman $\\rho$ and linear $R^2$ are annotated per panel.}\n\\label{fig:ddr-trends}\n\\end{figure*}\n\n\\subsection*{UMAP Embedding}\n\nFigure~\\ref{fig:umap} shows a 2D UMAP projection of the 7-feature space (7{,}500 stratified samples, StandardScaler normalization, $k$\\,=\\,30 neighbors, min\\_dist\\,=\\,0.3).\nPanel~(a) reveals a depth gradient from gen-0 through gen-4, with progressive overlap at deeper generations consistent with the convergence that motivates the gen-2+ class collapse.\nPanel~(b) shows the 3-class view: gen-0 forms a separable cluster, while gen-1 and gen-2+ overlap substantially, explaining the classification difficulty at this boundary.\n\n\\endgroup\n\n\\begin{figure}[H]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth, trim=0 0 356pt 0, clip]{fig_appendix_umap.pdf}\\\\[-2pt]\n{\\small (a) Colored by raw generation depth (0--4)}\\\\[6pt]\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth, trim=356pt 0 0 0, clip]{fig_appendix_umap.pdf}\\\\[-2pt]\n{\\small (b) Colored by 3-class label}\n\\caption{UMAP 2D projection of the 7-feature space ($N$\\,=\\,7{,}500 stratified sample). (a) A gradient structure emerges, with deeper depths converging. (b) Gen-0 separates clearly; gen-1 and gen-2+ overlap, consistent with the high false-negative rate at the gen-1/gen-2+ boundary.}\n\\label{fig:umap}\n\\end{figure}\n",
